### Added 2026-09-22 (F1 staging CLEANUP, Layer A)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P73 | **Before renaming or extracting a literal in a file that a GATE greps, read the gate's own predicates for that literal.** Measure it; do not reason about it. | Cleanup is behaviour-preserving *to the product* and can still turn a green gate red, because a shape row asserts on the source text, not on the behaviour. Here `H-1..H-6` were read before the first edit and none asserts on `'not reached'`, `120` or `500` — so the extraction was safe. Had `H-5` grepped the sentinel instead of `REQUIRED_CHECKS`, a tidy-up with no product effect would have taken Layer A from `FAILS=0` back to red for nothing. **P22/M34 is the same shape for diagrams; this is it for gates.** |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M63 | The **P70** "run it against a harmless target" check was first pointed at `http://127.0.0.1:9`. Chromium refuses port 9 as an **unsafe port** (`net::ERR_UNSAFE_PORT`) *before opening any socket*, so the run exercised a browser-policy rejection rather than the network path a real staging outage takes. | **None. Nothing false was recorded.** The error string itself names the cause on its face, and it was re-run in the same turn. The verdict happened to be identical either way (12 checks recorded, 0 `not reached`, 4 surfaces attempted) — but it was identical **by luck**, not by demonstration. | Re-run against an ordinary high closed port, `127.0.0.1:45999`, confirmed closed with `lsof` first: `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`, same 12/0/4 result. That is the measurement claimed in 6.4. | **"Dead target" is not one thing.** A blocked port, a refused port, an unroutable host and a black-holed host reach the error path through different code. When the whole point of the run is to observe a failure path, name which failure you actually produced — and check the error string says what you assumed it would. |
| M64 | The final sweep printed `git rev-list --count origin/development..HEAD` as **6**, while this session had already written "**FIVE** commits off `origin/development`" into the feature `HANDOFF.md`, the STATE FILE's `Repo changes` block and its section 0 handoff. The entry state was 4 ahead and this session added 2. | **None to the work, and it was caught before the session closed** — but it had already been COMMITTED in `260914d`, so it is a wrong fact that briefly existed in the repository, not merely a wrong sentence in chat. A next session reading "five" and finding six would have to stop and re-derive which document was lying. | Corrected in all three places and re-counted with `git rev-list --count` rather than by adding up remembered commits. The `Repo changes` block had listed all six SHAs directly above the word "FIVE", which is what exposed it. | **M26 again — and then a second, sharper lesson within minutes.** Count from the command, never from memory; when a document lists the items and then states their number, read the list back before trusting it. But the fix itself taught the real lesson: committing the correction made the count **7**, so the corrected file was wrong again the instant it was committed. **A commit count cannot live in a file that is part of what it counts** — it is self-invalidating, exactly as **P40** says a merge SHA cannot live in the commit it names. The number was removed from `HANDOFF.md` and replaced with the command that derives it. The first fix was right about the arithmetic and wrong about where the fact belongs. |

#### Standing assumptions (continued)

| # | Assumption | Confidence and caveat |
|---|---|---|
| A115 | At the cleanup commit `3fc3de4` the three tiers read **exactly** what they read at `4638004` before the edit: `staging-harness` **`FAILS=0`, exit 0, `ASSESSED=6`**; `local` **`FAILS=0`, exit 0, `ASSESSED=11`**; `staging` **`FAILS=4`, exit 4, `ASSESSED=5`**. **CONTROL reads FAIL in all three.** | Observed 2026-09-22, one run per tier before the edit and one after, compared **by string equality of the score line** rather than by eye. The staging tier being unchanged is the positive result that proves Layer A did not leak into Layer B — the same check **A114** made for the same reason. |
| A116 | **Chromium refuses a fixed list of "unsafe ports" before any socket is opened**, port 9 (discard) among them, with `net::ERR_UNSAFE_PORT`. An ordinary high port with nothing listening gives `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` instead. | Observed 2026-09-22, both strings read out of the harness's own `VERIFY.json` details. This is the whole of **M63**, and it matters to any future P70-style run: pick the port deliberately, and read the error to confirm which path you got. |
| A117 | **The gate reads the repository through `git show`/`git cat-file` at `$R`, never the working tree** (`f1-proof.sh` header). So an uncommitted cleanup edit is invisible to every shape row, and a tier re-run before committing scores the *old* bytes and will agree with the baseline for the wrong reason. | Observed 2026-09-22 by reading the gate's own header comment before relying on it. The edit was therefore committed first and the tiers re-run at the new HEAD. A session that re-ran the tier on a dirty tree and read "unchanged" as proof would have proved nothing at all. |


### Added 2026-09-22 (F1 staging VERIFICATION, Layer A)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P74 | **After an Extract-Constant refactor, mutating the constant's VALUE is no longer a valid behavioural probe.** The seed and every comparison read the same binding, so a value change moves them in lockstep and the artifact is byte-identical. Use a **decoupling** mutation instead: make one reader disagree with the others. | This verification's own brief prescribed the value mutation and it produced *no change whatsoever* — a byte-identical `VERIFY.json`. A critic that stopped there would have reported "mutation produced no change" and could reasonably have concluded the constant was dead code, which is the opposite of the truth. The decoupling mutation shows the path is load-bearing: `recIfUnreached()` stops firing and **11 of 12 checks lose their real failure reason**. The probe that works against the code you are *about to* write is often not the probe that works against the code you *have* written. |
| P75 | **When a builder writes the brief for its own critic, name that as a limitation of the verification in the verification itself**, and make the brief demand findings *beyond* it. | 2.1. A blind spot in the builder becomes a blind spot in the brief, and the reader of a PASS has no way to see that unless it is written down. Here it materialised immediately: the prescribed mutation could not work, and the only reason it was caught is that the critic was told to look past the list. Six of the most useful findings came from that instruction, not from the twelve criteria. |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M65 | The CLEANUP log and `HANDOFF.md` both said the five docs gates "were re-run at the **exact final HEAD** `3fc3de4`". Two further commits landed afterwards, so the sentence was one and then two commits stale. | **None to the results** — the gates really did pass at every commit including the true final HEAD, and the critic re-ran all six itself to confirm. But a reader re-deriving "the exact final HEAD" would have found the named commit was not it. | The commit is no longer named in either file; both now say the gates ran at every commit including the final one, and point at `git rev-parse HEAD`. | **M64 one layer out, and M64 did not generalise.** M64 learned that a *count* cannot live in a file that changes it. The same is true of any phrase naming "the HEAD": it is self-invalidating the moment another commit lands. The class is "facts about the tip, written into the tip". |
| M66 | CLEANUP's refactor analysis asserted "no nesting deeper than the `try`/`for`/`if` a linear script needs" — and never measured it. | The file reaches depth **8-9**, with roughly 14-15 lines at depth >= 7, against the repository's own coding-style rule of >4. The claim was not merely imprecise, it **denied a smell existed** in the very document whose job was to enumerate the smells. Deep nesting was therefore missing even from the "considered and deliberately skipped" list. | Claim removed; deep nesting is now a **classified, offered, not-patched** item with its reason — extracting the `page.evaluate` callback changes what runs in the browser on the one run this project pays for. | **An analysis phase may conclude "there is nothing here", but only about things it measured.** Every other smell in that paragraph had been checked; this one was asserted because it *felt* true of a linear script. The word "no" in an analysis is a measurement claim. |

#### Standing assumptions (continued)

| # | Assumption | Confidence and caveat |
|---|---|---|
| A118 | **The `NOT_REACHED` path is load-bearing, proved by decoupling.** With the comparison site made to disagree with the seed, `recIfUnreached()` stops firing and **11 of 12** checks lose their real failure reason and print the seeded sentinel instead; `diff` against the baseline `VERIFY.json` exits 1. With the constant's *value* changed instead, the output is **byte-identical** (`diff` exit 0). | Observed 2026-09-22 by the fresh critic, both mutations working-tree only and both reverted with `git checkout --`, worktree blob re-confirmed equal to `HEAD:tests/e2e/staging/run.mjs` afterwards. This is **P74**, and it is also the behavioural evidence CLEANUP itself did not produce (**8.C** of that log). |
| A119 | **`WINDOW_MS = 60_000` names a FLOOR, not the window.** The loop is 120 sleeps of 500 ms **plus** 120 `page.evaluate()` round trips, so the real wall-clock window is 60 s + sampling overhead. The harness prints the true elapsed at `:232`, so a reader will see a number larger than 60 beside a constant asserting 60. `SAMPLE_MS` likewise names the *sleep*, not the sample period. | Observed 2026-09-22. **Offered, not patched** — renaming is cosmetic churn on a file whose next run costs money. Worth knowing before anyone budgets the paid Layer B run from the constant rather than from the printed elapsed. |
| A120 | At `6298fe8`, verified independently by a critic that built none of it: `staging-harness` **FAILS=0 exit 0 ASSESSED=6**; `local` **FAILS=0 exit 0 ASSESSED=11**; `staging` **FAILS=4 exit 4 ASSESSED=5**; CONTROL **FAIL** in all three. The five docs gates and `git diff --check` all exit 0 (docs-links 211 files / 3475 links; diagrams-drift 8 mapped diagrams; docs-assets 1.98 MB). `4638004..HEAD` is **three paths**, 0 production src. Nothing pushed; HEAD contained in **none** of the four tips. | Observed 2026-09-22, each exit code captured unpiped, tree confirmed clean before every tier. The harness-tier table body was additionally shown **byte-identical** across `4638004`, `3fc3de4` and `6298fe8`, which is a stronger statement than three matching `FAILS=` lines. |
| A121 | **`tests/e2e/staging/run.mjs` reaches nesting depth 8-9**, with ~14-15 lines at depth >= 7, inside the `page.evaluate` callback nested `frames.push` -> `for` -> `if` -> `try` -> `for` -> `try`. The repository's own common coding-style rule flags anything past 4. | Observed 2026-09-22, measured twice by different methods (the critic's structural read and the coordinator's brace count) with results one apart — 8 vs 9, 15 vs 14 — which is the expected disagreement between two approximations and does not change the conclusion. **Classified debt, offered and NOT patched** (**M66**). |


### Added 2026-09-22 (F1 staging LANDING, Layer A)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P76 | **An expired authorization window is never standing permission.** When a landing relies on a new directive instead, **quote the words you are relying on and record the reading in writing BEFORE the first push**, in the log that ships with the change. | Two windows had expired here, and the only live authorization was a general directive rather than a yes to this specific push. A reading like that is a judgement, and a judgement that is never written down is indistinguishable afterwards from an assumption nobody checked. Section 2.1 quotes the three phrases relied on and states the scope the reading does *not* extend to, so a reader who disagrees can see exactly what was decided and object to the decision rather than having to reconstruct it. |
| P77 | **Run the full pre-push list on the commit you will actually push, which means committing the session log FIRST and running it again.** The log commit changes the HEAD, and the HEAD is what CI receives. | **P51.** A list that passed on the commit *before* the write-up is not a list that passed on what was pushed. This project has run it twice at every landing for that reason, and the second run is the one that matches the SHA in the pull request. |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M67 | This landing log and the feature `HANDOFF.md` were written **in the past tense** — "Landed F1's staging tier into `development`" — and **committed before the push was attempted**. The push was then refused by the execution environment's permission layer, so for the life of one commit both documents asserted a landing that had not happened. | **Contained, and caught in the same session** — but it is exactly the failure this repository's honesty rules exist to prevent (**P2**: never record a command as passing unless it actually ran). A reader of `7f857e4` alone would have believed the work was on GitHub. Nothing downstream consumed the false claim. | Both documents corrected in the same session, in a follow-up commit that says plainly what happened; **6.4** records the blocked push and the decision not to route around it. | **The log has to be committed before the push — but it must not be written as though the push succeeded.** P77 is right that the write-up has to be inside the commit CI receives; the mistake was tense, not sequence. Write the pre-push state in the past tense and the landing itself in the *future* tense, then correct it afterwards with the outcome. "What I am about to do" and "what I did" are different sentences, and only one of them can be committed before the fact. |

#### Standing assumptions (continued)

| # | Assumption | Confidence and caveat |
|---|---|---|
| A124 | **A harness-level permission denial is not a Gauntlet gate and cannot be satisfied by better evidence.** The `git push` here was refused by the execution environment, outside this repository and outside the Gauntlet. Section 2.1's authorization reading may be entirely correct and the push is still refused. | Observed 2026-09-22. Two consequences worth carrying. First, **do not re-argue it** — a denial is not a failing test that a stronger proof turns green. Second, **do not route around it**: performing the same action through different tooling bypasses the denial's intent rather than finding a safer method, and that is a line this loop does not cross. The correct response is to park with a report and hand it to the owner. |
| A122 | **The remote is `https://github.com/Adams-Captial-Holdings-Ltd/Netzer-Labs.git`**, while `development.md:25` still names `emmanuelmadams4-lgtm/Netzer-Labs`. | Re-confirmed 2026-09-22 from `git remote -v`. This is **A77**'s third item and it remains **offered, not patched** — `development.md` may not be edited without the owner's approval for that specific change. Worth knowing before anyone follows that line to find the repository. |

### Added 2026-09-22 (F1 Layer B, the first authorized staging run)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P78 | **Before an automated deep link into any product, seed that product's first-run state.** A fresh browser profile is not a neutral starting point — it is a *first-time user*, and products treat first-time users differently. Set the keys in `addInitScript`, before any page script runs, the same way the theme already is. | **M68.** `App.tsx` redirects to `/getting-started` when neither onboarding key is in `localStorage`, whatever URL was requested. Every automated run is a fresh profile **by design**, so this fired on 100% of runs and made every deep link land on the onboarding checklist. It cost the first authorized staging run its two most valuable checks. |
| P79 | **When work moves, move the gate's read path in the same change, and prove the gate can still see the work.** | **M69.** Owner decision 38 moved the harness into the repository; the gate's `S-c`/`S-d`/`S-e` kept reading the devkit archive's `bar/staging/VERIFY.json`, a snapshot of an **August** run. A *perfect* authorized run would still have scored `FAILS=4`, because the gate was not looking at it. A gate reading a file the work no longer writes is worse than no gate: it returns a stale answer with a confident face. |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M68 | The ported harness assumed that navigating to a URL lands you on it. **P72 says: when you port a check, re-derive EVERY assertion it makes against the product.** The selectors were re-derived. The auth was re-derived. **The navigation was not.** | **The first authorized staging run — the one this project waited on since August — reached 10 of 12 and could not reach the two checks it exists for.** Sign-in and the roll-call passed; the browser was on `/dashboard/getting-started` the whole time. Recoverable only because it cost nothing: the message is sent inside the skipped block, so no model turn ever ran. | The onboarding key is set in `addInitScript` beside the theme. Measured before and after: **without it, `/dashboard/getting-started`, 0 composers; with it, `/dashboard/chat`, 2 composers.** | **P72 names the trap and I walked into it anyway, because "go to the page" does not feel like an assertion.** It is one. Every `goto` claims the destination will be the destination, and a product with any first-run, paywall, consent or redirect logic can refuse that claim silently — the page still loads, still renders, still passes a screenshot check. |
| M69 | The gate's `S-b..S-e` read `$QA` — the devkit **archive** — after owner decision 38 moved the harness into the repository. | **A perfect run could never have scored.** On 2026-09-22 the real run passed sign-in on all four surfaces and the bearer roll-call against real Bedrock, and every one of those rows was still scoring a 2026-09-17 snapshot of an August run with 5 entries and 4 failed sign-ins. | `S-b` now reads the in-repo harness and `S-c`/`S-d`/`S-e` read `$REPO/tests/e2e/staging/out/VERIFY.json`, with a `[ -f ]` guard so "no run yet" scores as a clean fail rather than a `require()` crash. | **A gate is a reader, and a reader has an address.** When the work moves, the gate does not follow by itself — and nothing about a stale read looks wrong from the outside, because the numbers still print. |

|---|---|---|

The first probe ran with **no backend** and found 0 composers on *both* sides — the redirect fix was proved, but composer presence was not, so the orchestrator was started and the probe re-run rather than the gap being papered over. Both numbers above come from the second run. |

It does NOT prove they pass on staging — only a real run does that. |

So the run that found three defects cost effectively nothing against the $25/day budget — the first real spend is still ahead. |

### Added 2026-09-22 (P1 SPECIFICATION)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P80 | **A gate row that runs a NAME-FILTERED test must require a test to have actually run, not merely a zero exit.** `npx vitest run <file> -t "<name nobody has written>"` exits **0** and reports the tests as skipped. Require a `passed` **or** `failed` count on vitest's own `Tests` summary line, and keep the two apart. | Measured 2026-09-22 before this gate's first run. Every future row of `p1-proof.sh` was written as a `-t` filter naming a test IMPLEMENTATION has yet to write — so scored on the exit code alone, **each one would have read PASS for work nobody had done**. That is **M41/M48/P54 for the fifth time in this project**, and this time it would have been the whole gate rather than one row. Keeping "ran and failed" distinct from "never written" is **D4/M69** applied to a test filter: both score FAIL, and they call for completely different next actions. |
| P81 | **A `chk`-style row runs its predicate in a child `bash -c`, which inherits only EXPORTED functions.** Export every helper the rows use, and prove it with one positive control — a predicate that must PASS — before trusting any row. | **M71.** Without the export, an absence row (`! show X \| grep -q Y`) runs `show` as "command not found", greps empty input, and reads **PASS** on the unfixed tree; a presence row reads FAIL forever. The gate answers without looking, in both directions. `f1-proof.sh:81-82` does export its helpers — this was a new gate's omission, not a defect in the merged one. |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M70 | This session ran read-only commands before its Step-0 declaration: `wc`/`grep`/`sed`/`awk` over the STATE FILE (outside the repository) and one `ls -1t` over `docs/features/netzar-chat/handoffs/` **inside** it, to learn which log was newest. | None to the work — nothing changed and the declaration did not rest on either. It broke **P19**'s order again. | Disclosed in the Step-0 declaration itself, as M39 and M46 were. | **M39/M46, a third time, and the repeat is the finding.** The correction "do Step 0's reading with the Read tool only" keeps being defeated by the same thing: the handoff asks you to read a file it does not name the path of, or one too large to read whole, and locating it is a command. The durable fix is for a handoff to NAME the newest log, not to describe it. |
| M71 | `p1-proof.sh`'s first working draft did not export `show`/`saysE` to the child shell `chk` uses, and its `S-h` row therefore read **PASS at 3393f6c** — certifying that a comment had been corrected when the comment is right there at `handler.ts:3265`. | **A wrong PASS existed in a run I performed.** It was contained: the row was one of two that passed on an unfixed tree, and this session's own discipline is to inspect every such row (**P54**). Nothing downstream consumed it. | Both causes found and fixed. The export was added with a positive-control probe proving the same predicate returns FAIL before `export -f` and PASS after. `S-h` also matched a sentence that **wraps across two source lines** with a `//` between them, which `grep -F` can never match — narrowed to the one-line fragment `no orphan to sweep`, which the file really does contain (1 hit). | **Two independent ways for an absence row to pass on empty input, stacked in one row.** The habit that caught it is not vigilance about either bug: it is treating *any* row that PASSES on the unfixed tree as a defect until proved otherwise. A green row in a red gate is the thing to go and look at. |

Do not repeat the inference as though it were observed. |

It is not hotfixed: decision 9 is explicit that P1 fixes all routes against the owner model with no hotfix, and constraint 4 forbids patching it opportunistically. It is the strongest single argument for WI-1.1 and it is written up for the owner in plain words. |

Two consequences: a per-message record must share its conversation's partition (`SK=CHAT#<chatId>#MSG#…`, matching the repo's own `MEM#<chatId>#<iso>#<uuid>` convention at `dynamodb-tools.ts:64`), and **"list the conversations owned by user X" cannot become a Query** — it stays a scan-and-filter unless decision 11 is reopened with the owner. A durable `userId` already rides the principal for member sessions and is discarded — captured only as `dashboardUserId` for cost attribution (`:1941`, `:2278`), and absent entirely from `SessionEnrichment` (`sessions-repo.ts:261-264`). The env root token carries no `userId` at all (`:1023`). | Observed 2026-09-22. So token rotation orphans conversations, and **decision 1**'s requirement that root / no-`userId` tokens bind to one concrete immutable user has a concrete home: `dashboardIdentity`. |

|---|---|

| `grep -F` cannot match a comment sentence that wraps across two source lines with a `//` between them. `handler.ts:3265` holds `no orphan to sweep`; the full sentence starting on `:3264` matches nothing. | This log, **M71** |
| The sessions table has **no GSI**, so nothing can be queried by `userId`. `GET /sessions` is a `Scan` today and owner-scoped listing cannot become a Query without reopening decision 11. | This log, **A132** |
| `jarvis-memory-repo.ts` already writes `PK=JARVIS#…`, `SK=TURN#…` into `claudeclaw-sessions`. Any reader of that table that assumes every row is a conversation is already wrong, before P1 adds a single record. | This log, **A130** |


### Added 2026-09-22 (P1 WI-1.0 IMPLEMENTATION)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P82 | **When two reviewers of the same diff contradict each other on a load-bearing invariant, neither is the answer. Re-derive it yourself, at the source line, before accepting either.** | **M73.** The code reviewer said a legitimate `chatId` can contain `#`; the security reviewer said the one caller-influenced producer already strips it. Both were confident and one was wrong. Reading `safeDashboardChatId` settled it in thirty seconds: it returns an already-prefixed id **verbatim** and the sanitiser only runs on unprefixed ones. **M35 applied to fan-out**: a reviewer's finding is a lead, and so is a reviewer's *reassurance* — the reassurance is the more dangerous of the two, because nothing about it invites checking. |
| P83 | **A control that reverts a file in the WORKING TREE cannot test a gate that reads COMMITTED history. Make the control a throwaway commit, then `git reset --hard` back to the recorded SHA.** | **M72.** `diagrams-drift.mjs` diffs `origin/<base>...HEAD`. Reverting the diagram in the working tree and re-running it reported "no drift across 6 changed file(s)" — which reads exactly like "the diagram edit was unnecessary". It was measuring the commit, which still had the edit. **A117 is the same fact about `p1-proof.sh`**; this is the general rule, and it applies to every gate in this repository that reads git rather than the tree. |
| P84 | **A filter added to a read path fails silently in one direction. Before committing one, write the test for the direction that has no error message.** | An over-rejecting predicate drops a real row, and a dropped row is indistinguishable from one that never existed — no exception, no log, no count. The five tests written first all measured *under*-filtering, the direction that is loud. The sixth measures over-filtering, and it is the only one that would have caught the `#` defect had the predicate been one character wider. |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M72 | The first drift-gate control reverted `docs/diagrams/architecture-security.md` in the **working tree** and re-ran `diagrams-drift.mjs`, which reported **exit 0, "no drift"**. Read literally, that says the mapped-diagram edit this session had just made was not required. | **None. Nothing false was recorded.** The result was implausible on its face — a gate whose entire job is to fail on exactly this shape had just declined to — and the script's own `origin/development...HEAD` diff was re-read in the same turn. Re-run as a throwaway commit it FAILS by name: *"docs/diagrams/architecture-security.md may have rotted: this PR changes src/adapters/dashboard/handler.ts without touching the diagram."* | The control is now a real commit, reset afterwards to the recorded SHA with `git reset --hard`, HEAD and a 0-line porcelain both re-checked. | **M52/M57/M59 in a new place: a check that reads the wrong input returns a number, not an error.** What saved it here is the same thing that saved those — the answer was the *convenient* one, and a convenient answer from a control is the one to distrust. A control that cannot fail is not a control, and a control aimed at the wrong artifact cannot fail. |
| M73 | The predicate's own comment, the commit message and the diagram bullet all asserted that "a chatId carries no `#`", citing `lib/tools/dynamodb-tools.ts:63`'s `MEM#<chatId>#<iso>#<uuid>` as an existing invariant. **That line is a tool *description string*, not a validator, and nothing anywhere enforces it.** | **A real defect in this session's own change, caught before the phase closed but after it was committed.** Two producers can emit a `#`-bearing chatId (A137, A138), and the predicate silently drops the resulting conversation from all four readers. Nothing is pushed or deployed, so no live listing is affected. | The claim is withdrawn from this log and the hazard is put to the owner in 8 as the one genuine fork. The predicate is **left strict** rather than quietly widened: widening it to a `#MSG#` blocklist would weaken a release gate the owner defined, which is precisely the opportunistic patch **constraint 4** forbids. A sixth test pins the legitimate shapes so the next change cannot narrow it further unnoticed. | **"The repo already assumes this" is a claim about the repo, and it needs the same evidence as any other.** A convention documented in prose, a comment, or a tool description is a convention nobody validates. Before resting a predicate on an invariant, find the line that would REJECT a violation of it — and if there is no such line, the invariant is a wish. |

So `dashboard:a#b`, supplied in a send-route request body, is stored verbatim as `SK=CHAT#dashboard:a#b`. | Observed 2026-09-22 by reading the whole function after the two reviewers disagreed (**P82**). **This corrects the security review's "safeDashboardChatId already strips `#`"**, which is true only of the branch that does not fire for prefixed ids. Whether any real Paperclip issue id contains `#` is **Unknown**: answering it needs a read against real data, which is not authorized here. |

Arithmetic against the baseline closes: 247 files + this file = 248; 3215 tests + this file's 6 = 3221. **P41 DOES NOT HOLD and is not claimed** — see A144. |

| A141 | **The fix is load-bearing, proved by two mutations, not by reading it.** With `isConversationRow` neutralised to `return true`, the suite is **4 failed \| 1 passed**. With only the `[^#]+$` anchor relaxed to a bare `/^CHAT#/` — the naive fix a reviewer might wave through — it is **also 4 failed \| 1 passed**, and the four are named in the log. | A143 | **A fifth reader of `claudeclaw-sessions` exists and is deliberately out of scope**: the model-facing `dynamodb_query` tool (`lib/tools/dynamodb-tools.ts:196-221`). It returns rows as `JSON.stringify(...).slice(0, 500)` text and never maps them into a conversation shape, so decision 11's "accepts solely `AGENT#…`/`CHAT#…`" does not govern it. Its `PK` scoping also cannot reach a `JARVIS#` partition. | Observed 2026-09-22, raised independently by the code review and re-derived here. **A30** already recorded that this tool can return raw session rows without `getSession`. It WILL return `MSG#` rows once WI-1.2 lands — as raw rows, labelled as such, which is what it is for. Classified, not patched. |
| A144 | **P41 does not hold for any measurement in this session, and is not claimed.** Two orphaned `tsx watch --env-file=.env src/index.ts` trees from earlier sessions were live in the checkout throughout, and the `kill` was **REFUSED by the execution environment** — the same denial as **A124**, not re-argued and not routed around. They then **recompiled and restarted the orchestrator in response to this session's edits**: a third process appeared and took `*:3000 (LISTEN)`, where nothing had been listening at session start. | Observed 2026-09-22, `pgrep` and `lsof` both unpiped (**M59**). Why the numbers are still trustworthy, stated as an argument and not as sole occupancy: `tsx watch` compiles in memory and writes nothing to the tree (`status-after: 0 changed paths`, twice), it runs no `npm run build` — the specific contention **P41** was written for — and `npm test` is hermetic. What it *can* touch is **Floci**, which the integration suite also reads; that suite came back at the baseline 38/25/1 with the gap named, so no drift was observed. The owner is asked to clear the processes in 8. |

#### Known traps that cost time (continued)

| Trap | Where the detail lives |
|---|---|
| A gate that reads `git` rather than the working tree cannot be probed by editing the working tree. Reverting a file and re-running the gate returns a confident PASS that measures the commit you did not change. Use a throwaway commit. | This log, **M72** / **P83**; **A117** is the same fact about `p1-proof.sh` |
| `safeDashboardChatId` returns an already-prefixed chatId **verbatim**; its sanitiser never runs on `dashboard:`/`system:`/`smoke:`/`test:` ids. Do not read the presence of a sanitiser in a function as proof that every path through it sanitises. | This log, **A137** |
| `MEM#<chatId>#<iso>#<uuid>` at `dynamodb-tools.ts:63` is a **tool description string**. It documents a key shape; it validates nothing. Several session logs cite it as an invariant. | This log, **M73** |
| The zsh `--include=*.md` glob trap recurred as `--include=*.ts`: unquoted, zsh expands it before `grep` sees it and the command dies with `no matches found`. Quote it. Same family as M27/M28. | This log, 7.2; first recorded in the WI-0.4 cleanup log |

### Added 2026-09-22 (P1 WI-1.0 VERIFICATION)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P88 | **To prove one conjunct of a predicate is load-bearing, replace it with the value that REMOVES it — its identity element, `true` inside an `&&`, `false` inside an `\|\|` — never with the value that removes the WHOLE predicate.** A constant-false predicate fails every positive test, which looks decisive and says nothing about the conjunct. | **M76 / A153.** Mutation 3 set `isConversationRow`'s `PK` half to `false`: all six tests failed, the control included, so it proved the suite notices over-rejection in general. The identity probe, `true`, is the one that asks the real question — and the answer was that no test pins that half. |
| P89 | **Score a ledger carry by the TYPE of its diff hunks — only `a`, zero `<` lines — never by their COUNT.** | Where `diff` aligns one unchanged blank line decides whether an appended block is one hunk or two: the cleanup log recorded its carry as "2 hunks" and the same diff measures 3 here, both additions-only. A critic comparing counts would have reported a discrepancy that does not exist (**A159**). |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M76 | **The IMPLEMENTATION log recorded mutation 3 — `isConversationRow`'s `PK` half replaced by `false` — as "the over-rejection test is the failure"** (its 6.4 and its section 7 table), and the state file repeated it. Measured by this critic: **all six** tests fail, the control included, because `false && …` rejects every row. | **None to the code or to any gate** — the mutation is caught, so the predicate as a whole was never in doubt. But the record names one test as *the* failure when all six are, and it reads as though the `PK` half were pinned. It is not (**A153**). | Re-run and recorded exactly (7.2): `Tests 6 failed (6)`, containment `FAILS=5`. The IMPLEMENTATION log is append-only history and is not edited; this row is the correction. | **P88.** Before recording what a mutation proved, name what it *can* prove. A mutation that makes a predicate constant tests the suite's direction, not any one of the predicate's parts. |

### Added 2026-09-22 (P1 WI-1.0 LANDING)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P90 | **A landing runs the full step 7 list ONCE, on the commit it will push — after its own log is committed — not once before and once after.** Run the docs gates on the working tree before the log commit instead: a Markdown-only log commit can break nothing else the list reads. | The F1 landings ran the list at the entry HEAD and again on the log commit (**P51/P77**): two full runs, of which only the second scores the commit CI receives. The entry run proves nothing about the pushed commit that the verification's own run had not already proved, and **A160** is the cheap way to show the code under it has not moved since. This landing's handoff ordered the log commit first and one run after it. |
| P91 | **When step 7 runs on a clean, committed HEAD — as every landing does (P77) — also run `git diff --check origin/development...HEAD`.** The list's own `git diff --check` compares the working tree with the index, so on a clean tree it reads nothing and exits 0 whatever the branch commits. | **A162.** Proved in a throwaway repository, and no workflow under `.github/workflows` runs a whitespace check at all, so on a landing that one line is the only whitespace check there is, and it checks nothing. Running more than the documented list is the coordinator's choice; changing the list is not — the wording of step 7 is a gap report for the owner (6.4), not an edit. |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M77 | One command chained this log's copy into the repository behind the assembly script — `bash assemble.sh && cp …; echo "cp_exit=$?"`. The script's LAST line greps for the section 7.1 placeholders, which had just been filled in, so it exited 1; `&&` skipped the copy; and the `echo` printed the ASSEMBLY script's status under the copy's name. The docs gates in the same command then ran on the previous version of this log. | **None recorded.** The same output showed the staged file at **1,737** lines where the assembly had just produced **1,764**, and that contradiction exposed it in the same turn. No number from that gate run was used. | Copied again as its own command with its exit code read directly, compared byte for byte with the assembled file (`cmp`), and every docs gate re-run on the right bytes (7.1). | **M13 in a new shape.** A label on an exit code is a claim about which command produced it, and `&&` moves that claim silently. Read each command's status right after it runs, and do not chain an action behind a script whose last line is a check that is allowed to fail. What caught it was the pairing again: a count in the output that disagreed with a count already known. |

> Anything learned after this commit — at the push, in CI, at the merge — cannot be recorded in it
> (**P40**) and goes to the Gauntlet state file.

#### Standing assumptions (continued)

| # | Assumption | Confidence and caveat |
|---|---|---|
| A160 | **The bytes this landing pushes are the bytes VERIFICATION scored.** `git diff --quiet a5c377b 10916c2 -- src infra web scripts tests docs/diagrams package.json package-lock.json vitest.config.ts vitest.setup.ts tsconfig.json .github` exits **0**; `a5c377b..10916c2` is exactly two paths, both Markdown under `docs/features/netzar-chat/` (the verification log and `HANDOFF.md`); and `handler.ts`, `jarvis.ts`, `sessions-containment.test.ts` and `architecture-security.md` have the same blob at both commits (`74a4f96c9209`, `ab19de6411fc`, `4f909011e475`, `704be63c6d1d`). | Observed 2026-09-22. **P30 applied to a landing**: relying on a verification's result needs the scoping diff that licenses it, and a statement of what that diff does not cover — here the Markdown under the feature folder, which the docs gates read and which this log's own commit adds to. |
| A161 | **A sole-occupancy check run from INSIDE the checkout finds itself.** `lsof -nP -d cwd` filtered by the clone's path lists the check's own `bash` and the `lsof` process — two lines, exit 0 — when nothing else is there at all. | Observed 2026-09-22; both PIDs were the check's own. The verification session filtered `lsof -d cwd` by process name (`node`, `tsx`, `vite`, `vitest`, `esbuild`), which excludes them; a path-only filter does not. Read the process names before calling a hit occupancy, or exclude the check's own process tree. |
| A162 | **`git diff --check`, as step 7 writes it, is vacuous on a clean committed tree.** In a throwaway repository outside the checkout, with a committed line ending in a space: `git diff --check` exits **0**; `git diff --check HEAD~1 HEAD` exits **2** and names `f.md:2: trailing whitespace.` No workflow under `.github/workflows` runs a whitespace check (`grep` exit 1, 0 lines). On this branch the committed range was checked directly: `git diff --check origin/development...HEAD` exit 0, 0 bytes; and with this landing's two files staged, `git diff --check origin/development` exit 0, 0 bytes. | Observed 2026-09-22. So a "`git diff --check` exit 0" recorded from a step 7 run on a committed HEAD is not evidence about the branch. This branch's committed range is clean; earlier branches were **not** re-checked, so nothing is claimed about them. **P43/P69** in a new place: a gate row that cannot fail. Offered to the owner as a **fourth A77 item** for `development.md` — **not patched**, because that page needs approval for the specific change. |

#### Known traps that cost time (continued)

| Trap | Where the detail lives |
|---|---|
| A path-filtered `lsof -d cwd` over the checkout always reports the check's own shell and `lsof` when the check runs inside it. A non-empty result is not occupancy until the process names are read. | This log, 3 and **A161** |
| zsh refuses to run a command whose glob matches nothing (`no matches found`, exit 1) instead of passing the pattern through as bash does, so `ls a b missing*` skips its other, matching arguments too, and the exit code is zsh's, not `ls`'s. The `--include=*.md` / `--include=*.ts` trap's family, a third form. Quote the pattern, or run it from a bash script file. | This log, 7 |


### Added 2026-09-22 (P1 ONE_SHOT)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P92 | **A recommendation a handoff carries from BEFORE a measurement is a lead once the measurement exists.** Re-derive it from the new evidence, and when it changes, tell the owner the old pick, the new pick and why — never carry it forward unexamined, never switch it silently. | Owner question 2 (`#` chat ids) arrived with "recommend (c)". Decision 46's count then measured **0** `#` ids in production, which made (a) both cheaper and more complete: (c) would have kept a `#` conversation hidden from every list. The owner was shown both picks and the reason, and chose (a) — decision 47. |
| P93 | **`git switch -c <branch> origin/<base>` sets `origin/<base>` as the new branch's upstream.** Unset it at once (`git branch --unset-upstream`), before the first commit: a bare `git push` would otherwise aim at the base branch. | This session's branch picked up `origin/development` as its upstream on creation. It was unset in the next command; nothing had been committed. |
| P94 | **An idempotency test ("the second run changes nothing") must also prove the FIRST run changed something, and an assertion over every item of a collection must first assert the collection is not empty.** A do-nothing implementation passes both otherwise. | Caught three times this session, each on a red-state stub BEFORE the red was trusted: the migration's second-run test and a "records carry no ttl" loop over zero records both passed on do-nothing code, and a CSV-export fixture sat below the 10,000-item ceiling it was written to trip. **M41/M48/P54 again** — the stub is where these show, so run the red against a stub, not against a missing export. |
| P95 | **When a unit-test double does not evaluate what the code relies on — condition, update or filter expressions — pin the command's SHAPE at unit level and prove the BEHAVIOUR against Floci in an `*.integration.test.ts`.** | `src/__test-utils__/fake-dynamo.ts` applies Puts and Deletes but neither update expressions nor conditions. So `/newchat`'s untouched counter is pinned by the command at unit level and proved end to end on Floci, and the all-or-nothing transaction is proved only on Floci (`sessions-repo.p1.integration.test.ts`). |
| P96 | **A check that refuses a row with no owner turns every writer that can CREATE such a row into a lockout.** Before adding the refusal, grep every writer of that key — Updates too: DynamoDB's `UpdateItem` creates a missing item — and make each one stamp the owner or refuse to create. | The first critic's **F2**: `clearSession`, `setModelOverride` and `setWarroomMode` upserted owner-less rows, and the send routes' new check then refused each to its own author for good (**M81**). |
| P97 | **An access rule for some data is owed by EVERY reader of that data, the model-facing tools included.** List the readers from a grep of the table, then scope each one or name it in the docs as an exception — never leave one out because it "returns metadata only". | The first critic's **F1** (the Jarvis `sessions` action, which **A156** had already listed as a reader) and **A1** (the agent `dynamodb_query` tool, which P1's records turned into a transcript reader) (**M80**). |
| P98 | **An audit that records a privileged READ is awaited and fails closed before the data leaves.** The repository's "turn-time audits stay fire-and-forget" is for turns; an override nobody can see recorded is the thing a hostile admin wants. | The first critic's **F4**: all four `session_read_override` writes were `void writeAudit(...)`, so the read succeeded with both audit stores down (**M83**). |
| P99 | **Whether a row expires is decided when it is created, and a later writer never arms an expiry on a durable row.** Any path that can is a data-loss path, and a job that skips expiring rows turns it into a silent one. | The first critic's **F3**: a person-less principal continuing a durable legacy conversation set its ttl, and the migration skips rows with a ttl (**M82**). |
| P100 | **A default that applies when a caller says nothing is a policy for EVERY caller that says nothing.** Before choosing it, grep the call sites and name each silent one; a rule stated as "settled once, never changed" is proved against every writer, not only the one that prompted it. | The second critic's **NEW-3**: `ephemeral` left unset defaulted to durable, and the scheduler and the queue drain set nothing (**M85**). |
| P101 | **A rule keyed on who created a row must say what happens when the creator is unknown.** A row created by a writer that names no owner is not settled by that writer; decide it when the first owner arrives, and make that arrival the thing tested. | The third critic's **C3-1**: "settled by whoever creates it" let an owner-less scheduled reply settle a person's new conversation as expiring (**M90**). |

#### Mistakes already made, and their corrections (continued)

| # | Mistake | Impact | Correction | Lesson |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M78 | The mutation runner was edited with a line-oriented `sed` that commented out only the FIRST line of a two-line mutation entry; bash then died on a syntax error at the orphaned second line. | **None.** The error came before any mutation ran, and `git status --porcelain` read 0 before the next step. | The one wanted mutation was extracted into its own script and run alone. The next runner (`mut-1.2.py`) selects mutations by LABEL passed as arguments. | A line-oriented edit of a multi-line construct leaves half of it behind. Choose what runs by name inside the runner, never by commenting out lines. |
| M79 | An anchored replacement for the sessions CSV export's `X-Export-Cap` header matched **three** routes — the audit, usage and sessions exports share that exact line. | **None.** The script asserts every anchor matches exactly once and stopped before writing, so no route changed. | Re-anchored on the lines only the sessions export has, its `Content-Disposition` filename among them. | **P73 applies to code as well as to gates**: a line that looks unique inside one route is often shared by its siblings. The exactly-once assertion is what made this free. |
| M80 | **A167** left the Jarvis `sessions` action unscoped: it returned metadata only, and scoping it would break WI-1.0's C-e fixture. | The first fresh critic **FAILED** `06a776d` on decisions 1 and 9 (**F1**): any Jarvis-enabled viewer listed every person's conversations. | Scoped in `bbee525` through the list's own helper; C-e now asks as its fixture's owner; A167 withdrawn (**P85**). | A reader the frozen specification names (its 3.6) is in scope, and a fixture the rule breaks is a fixture to fix — never a reason to leave a decision unmet (**P97**). |
| M81 | The send routes started refusing a row with no owner while three slash-command helpers still upserted owner-less rows. | A person whose first input in a new chat was `/newchat`, `/model` or `/warroom` was locked out of that chat for good (**F2**, proved on Floci by the critic). | `bbee525`: update only an existing conversation; `/warroom` creates one owned by its caller. | **P96**. |
| M82 | `appendTurns` armed a ttl on an existing durable row whenever the writer had no person. | The master token's five legacy chats could drop out of the migration and expire (**F3**). | `bbee525`: expiry settled at creation; re-owning re-owns and un-expires the records. | **P99**. |
| M83 | The four `session_read_override` audits were fire-and-forget. | An admin's read of someone else's conversation succeeded with both audit stores down (**F4**). | `bbee525`: `auditedOverride`, awaited before the data leaves; nothing returned when it fails. | **P98**. |
| M84 | The docs and diagrams said "every conversation route", "the same 404 as a missing one" and "their records stay". | **F5**: each was more than the code did. | `699a31b`: each route's actual answer, and the two readers still not owner-scoped, stated. | **P85** again: a claim about ALL routes needs the list of the routes it covers. |
| M85 | `bbee525` wrote "whether it expires is settled at creation" while two writers still changed it: a scheduled turn (no enrichment, so durable) and a person continuing a chat that began person-less (strip). | The second fresh critic **FAILED** `699a31b` on decisions 1 and 46 (**NEW-3**); **NEW-2** unmapped. | `089aba4`: the rule enforced for every writer, and the one behaviour it replaced retired with evidence (**A175**). | **P100**. |
| M86 | WI-1.2 numbered a new conversation from 0 without asking whether an expired predecessor's records still held those numbers. | A returning Telegram or Slack user's every message could fail after an idle week, until the sweep reached the leftovers (**NEW-1**, Floci-proven by the critic). | `089aba4`, `b0eb5b0`: records already past their own expiry are cleared on a clash; a live one never. | A key reused after a TTL expiry meets whatever the sweep has not reached: TTL deletes in no set order and with no documented bound on its lag. |
| M87 | The A1 fix filtered AFTER DynamoDB's `Limit`. | `dynamodb_query` under-reported conversation rows (**NEW-4**). | `089aba4`: reads on past hidden rows and says when it stopped short. | The WI-1.0 review's lesson — filtering after a bounded read under-reports — repeated in the fifth reader. |
| M88 | `bbee525` scoped the Jarvis `sessions` action by the principal without checking every way a Jarvis principal is built: the voice ticket carries no person. | Members saw none of their own conversations over voice (**NEW-7**; fails closed). | `089aba4`: the voice principal takes the person from the token's record, as the HTTP path does. | **P97** reaches every constructor of the principal too. |
| M89 | Two slips in round three's red run: a queued `mockResolvedValueOnce` survived `vi.clearAllMocks()` into the next test, and the voice test assumed one admission check per session (there are two, H15). | **None**: both showed as wrong failure reasons at the red, before it was trusted. | `mockReset` in that describe; each session's principal captured on its own. | `clearAllMocks` keeps queued once-answers; reset a mock whose queue a test may not drain (**P94**'s kin). |
| M90 | `089aba4` settled a conversation's lifetime at creation, and a scheduled reply saved before the first turn of its chat creates it with no owner. | The third fresh critic **FAILED** `0f5d7d1` on decisions 46 and 1 (**C3-1**); the critic gate's third straight FAIL fired the Three-Strike Breaker and stopped the session until the owner chose how to go on. | `ea6c809`: settled when it first has an owner; that arrival adopts the earlier records. | **P101**. And the fix for one mapped finding is where the next one hides: prove a new rule against every writer AND every order in which writers can arrive. |
| M91 | The HANDOFF rewrite at `0f5d7d1` left three WI-1.0 bullets beneath the new ones (248/3221 tests, storage `FAILS=8`, "the next work item is WI-1.1's specification", questions decisions 46 and 47 had answered), and the log kept "A143 … returns … message rows" after `bbee525` hid them. | The third critic's **C3-2** and **C3-3**, advisory. | This log's commit: the stale bullets replaced; the A143 line corrected. | A rewrite that keeps old bullets beneath new ones is two snapshots in one file: re-read the whole file at its own `as_of_commit`. |

#### Standing assumptions (continued)

| # | Assumption | Confidence and caveat |
|---|---|---|
| A163 | **Decision 40's count, on production** (`claudeclaw-sessions` + `claudeclaw-dashboard-tokens`, 2026-09-23T01:1xZ): 1,453 items scanned in one page; **10** conversation rows, **9** dashboard; **4** provable through a member-login token (1 owner), **5** owned by the root token (`createdBy: system`, 1 owner), all updated 2026-08-24..2026-09-14; **0** chat ids containing `#`. | Observed ONCE, read-only, the owner present and approving the exact command (decision 46). Script `netzar-chat-plan/p1-legacy-count.mjs` (sha256 `25a97f22…b0da`), dry-run first against throwaway Floci tables with the exact expected classification; output saved as `netzar-chat-plan/p1-legacy-count-result-2026-09-23.txt`. A snapshot, not a standing fact: the S1 migration's own dry run re-counts. |
| A164 | **Floci 1.6.0 serves `TransactWriteItems` all-or-nothing**: a transaction whose first item's condition fails writes NONE of its items and rejects with `TransactionCanceledException`, `CancellationReasons` `[ConditionalCheckFailed, None]`. | Observed 2026-09-22 on a throwaway table (`p1tx-probe`, deleted), then through the repository's own code: a taken sequence number fails a whole append, window included (`sessions-repo.p1.integration.test.ts`). LocalStack, which CI's integration lane uses, serves the same API; CI is the proof there. |
| A165 | **`aws login` attaches its session to the profile it is run with.** Run bare, it wrote `login_session` under `[default]`, so `netzer-process`'s `credential_process` (`aws configure export-credentials --profile netzer-root`) still found nothing. The repo's AWS SDK (`@aws-sdk/credential-provider-node` 3.972.54, with `credential-provider-login`) reads a `[default]` login session directly. | Observed 2026-09-22. Two attempts made NO data call before the owner said to run the command with `AWS_PROFILE=default` — same account, same root sign-in, same script. Next time: `aws login --profile netzer-root` keeps `netzer-process` working. |
| A166 | **`writeAudit` never throws**: it resolves the kill-switch check with a catch and hands the write to a fire-and-forget persist. `writeAuditOrThrow` is the awaited, throwing variant. | Read 2026-09-22 (`src/lib/audit/audit-writer.ts`). It is why the migration's audit after a delete cannot turn a finished delete into a reported failure. |
| A167 | **WITHDRAWN 2026-09-22 — the first critic's F1 mapped it to decision 9; scoped in `bbee525` (M80).** Was: **The Jarvis `sessions` action returns metadata only** — `agentId`, `chatId`, `updatedAt`, `messageCount`, `surface`; no text, no owner — so it was left NOT owner-scoped. Scoping it would also have broken WI-1.0's C-e fixture, whose conversation is an operator's while the action asks as a viewer. | Read 2026-09-22 (`jarvis.ts`). Classified, not patched. Revisit if the action ever returns `firstMessage` or text. |
| A168 | **Files under `src/__test-utils__/` compile into `dist/`**: `tsconfig.build.json` excludes only `*.test.ts` and `test-helpers.ts`. `localstack.ts` already shipped that way; `fake-dynamo.ts` (no imports, no side effects) now does too. | Read 2026-09-22. Harmless dead weight; classified, not patched — a build-config change is outside this step. |
| A169 | **Every guard P1 added is load-bearing, proved by mutation at UNIT level**: m1..m18 and the A153 identity probe (the `PK` half of `isConversationRow` set to `true`) each fail at least one test — after two gaps were found and closed: m3, the pre-link token-hash owner match (**0** failures until `4545f51`), and m18, the migration's version bump (caught only on Floci until `c88f8db`). | Observed 2026-09-22, each mutation in the working tree only, restored with `git checkout HEAD --`, 0 porcelain after every batch. |
| A170 | **`src/__test-utils__/fake-dynamo.ts` evaluates ONE kind of condition**: a lone key-existence check (`attribute_exists(PK)`, `attribute_not_exists(PK)`, the same on `SK`) on a standalone Put or Update, throwing `ConditionalCheckFailedException` as DynamoDB does. Update expressions, transactions' conditions, filters and paging stay unevaluated. | Added in `bbee525` so "update only if it exists" and "create only if missing" can be followed end to end at unit level. **P95** still governs the rest. |
| A171 | **Re-owning a conversation updates its existing records one by one BEFORE the re-own transaction** (owner, and defensively expiry — since `089aba4` no durable conversation should carry an expiring record), not inside it: the 100-item transaction limit cannot hold a long conversation. Idempotent; a failure leaves the conversation legacy for a re-run; a record that expired meanwhile is not brought back (`attribute_exists`). | Read and proved on Floci 2026-09-22 (mutation N14 caught only there). |
| A172 | **The agent `dynamodb_query` tool hides the per-message and source records since `bbee525`, but still returns the conversation rows of the calling agent's own partition** — every person's conversations with that agent, 500 characters each. Older than P1 (**A30**, **A143**, security finding #4). | Observed 2026-09-22. Whether owner decisions 1 and 9 cover agent tools is an owner question for a later step; P1 only stopped its own records from widening it. |
| A173 | **The admin delete override is audited as `data_purged`, fire-and-forget and suppressible by `audit_log_disabled`**, exactly as at `0178980`. Tripping that switch is itself the always-audited `kill_switch_set`, and each dropped write leaves an always-audited `audit_suppressed` marker (deduped per minute). | Read 2026-09-22 (`handler.ts`, `audit-writer.ts`). Older than P1 and deliberate ("never fail a delete the database has already committed"); classified, carried. |
| A174 | **`/warroom` creates a conversation owned by its caller, so a squatter can pre-create owned rows at guessed `dashboard:<epoch-ms>` ids without any model spend**; the person who later draws that id gets a 404. It needs a millisecond-exact guess. | Inferred 2026-09-22 from `web/src/lib/chat/helpers.ts` (the id the client mints). LOW, the first critic's A10 family; the fix is the client's id minting, outside P1. |
| A175 | **The pre-persistence ttl strip is retired** (`089aba4`). Dashboard persistence (`f132a9c`) reached staging on 2026-08-13 and production on 2026-08-14 (their first-parent merges `334765c` and `bb2898b`); since then no code writes a ttl on a dashboard row, so every ttl left by the older code has expired (7 days, plus a sweep lag DynamoDB documents no bound for but which a month-long margin covers) or been stripped by a later turn. After P1 a dashboard row carries a ttl only because a principal with no person owns it, or nobody owns it yet (**P101**). | Merge dates Observed 2026-09-23; that deploys followed those merges is Inferred (`development.md`: a merge to staging deploys). |
| A176 | **An expiring conversation's records each expire a week after they were written, not with the conversation**: the row's ttl is refreshed on every write, a record's never is. An active expiring chat's transcript is a rolling week of records plus the 20-turn window; before P1 it was the window alone. | Read 2026-09-23 (`sessions-repo.ts`). The P1 specification's "TTL parity per message record" was listed there as "not yet criteria". |
| A177 | **A scheduled task that finds no conversation starts one with no owner**, which expires. If the chat's first turn is still being saved, that turn's owner settles it (`ea6c809`); if not — the chat deleted, or its first turn never saved — it stays owner-less, only an admin can see it, and a recurring task keeps it alive. | Read and Floci-proved 2026-09-23. Before `089aba4` that row was permanent. |

#### Known traps that cost time (continued)

| Trap | Where the detail lives |
|---|---|
| A `vitest -t` filter is matched against the FULL test name (describe + it) in EVERY file under the path given, so a gate row's filter can be satisfied by a test in a sibling file. Grep the phrase under the path before the row's first run (**P80**'s companion). | This log, 6.5 |
| A `;` inside a mermaid SEQUENCE-diagram message is a statement separator: the message ends there and the parse fails. `docs-mermaid` catches it; write a comma. | This log, 7 |
| Tests in one integration file share one table, so a row one test leaves behind changes what a WHOLE-TABLE job — the legacy migration — sees in a later test. Clean up in `afterAll`. | This log, 7 |
| The sessions table now holds three shapes behind one conversation key: the conversation `CHAT#<id>`, its messages `CHAT#<id>#MSG#<seq>`, and a migrated conversation's source record `CHAT#<id>#SRC`. A new reader that lists conversations must go through `readConversationRows` / `isConversationRow`. | This log, 6.3; the feature README, section 6 |

### Added 2026-09-23 (P2 ONE_SHOT)

#### Process precautions (continued)

| # | Precaution | Why it exists |
|---|---|---|
| P102 | **When the skill and section 0 disagree on the same day, ask the owner before building; never pick one silently.** | Section 0's step 4 handoff said "Claude builds" and the gauntlet-loop skill, updated the same day, said Codex writes the code. Asked; the owner chose Codex (decision 49). |
| P103 | **A browser assertion about "during the stream" uses a short explicit timeout and a positive in-stream anchor** (Stop still showing). The chat suite's 30 s expect timeout outlasts a whole fixture, so a default-timeout check simply waits for the reply to end and passes for the wrong reason. | M94. |
| P104 | **A work order names the existing invariants its change must NOT break, each with a test that fails if it breaks.** | M98: work order A named none, and the first build broke four (a viewerless turn's fallback, the `invoking tool` log's args, an abort logged as an error, the 400 KB item limit). |
| P105 | **A new egress channel inherits every egress control of the old one** — the block redactor, the error sanitiser, access rules — and the work order names them. | M103: the tool panel carried raw tool payloads, secrets and other people's rows included, past the redactor the reply text always passed. |
| P106 | **Prove an abort against the dependency's REAL disconnect semantics** — the server stack's (does a disconnect before the body is read fire anything?) and the SDK's (does an aborted stream throw, or end?) — not only a unit test that cancels the body or throws `AbortError`. | M100, M101: both mocks were kinder than the real libraries. |
| P107 | **A proof row that says the CLIENT notices something needs a page-level test**, not only the parser's. | M104: `done: not_saved` and `errorCode` were parsed and then ignored by the page; the rows proved the parser. |
| P108 | **A large Opus review fan-out can exhaust the account's session window**: have each reviewer write its report to a file before its summary, and resume a cut-off reviewer by its id after the reset rather than re-running it fresh. | M106. |
| P109 | **A Codex run that exits 1 is read from its stdout before anything else**: "Your workspace is out of credits" is Codex's limit, which hands the build to Claude at the same height (decision 49) — not a builder failure to retry. Save the partial diff as a patch, restore the clean commit, then hand over. | M107. |
| P110 | **After a Claude takeover, the Codex inspection is not a formality — run it before the critic.** | This step: it found four material gaps that six reviewers, the builder and the coordinator had all passed (M108–M111). |
| P111 | **Test a new egress control with the REAL patterns on the channel's real format.** | M109: the redaction test mocked the redactor, and the JSON shape tool payloads carry slipped past the real pattern. |

| M92 | Two read-only commands (a `cat` of the memory files and an `awk` over the state file's headings) ran before the Step-0 reading of `development.md`. | **None**: read-only, nothing decided from them. | Disclosed in chat and here; the Step-0 reading then ran before any repository command. | Step 0 comes before ANY command, read-only included. |
| M93 | The E-3 draft test's title locator matched two buttons (the desktop rail and the hidden mobile drawer): a strict-mode violation, not the defect. | **None**: caught in the red run. | `fe3d216`: `button[title="…"]:visible`. | The conversation list renders twice; scope Playwright locators with `:visible`. |

| M95 | A red agent-loop test crashed on an unset `bedrockSend` (TypeError) instead of failing at its assertion. | **None**. | A harmless default `finalRound` mock. | A red test fails at the assertion that names the defect; every mock gets a harmless default. |
| M96 | The route's abort tests leaked a stopped turn's late save into the next test (`appendTurns` counted twice). | **None**: caught in the red run. | Per-test chat ids and `savesFor(chatId)`. | A save that happens after the request is gone lands after its test; key assertions by the test's own id. |
| M97 | Two red tests carried type errors (a mock's parameter type, a possibly-undefined `tools`) that the builder's typecheck caught. | **None**. | `b2b6954`, assertions unchanged. | Typecheck the red commit; only the expected "missing export" errors may remain. |
| M98 | Work order A named no existing invariant, and the first build broke four: a turn nobody watches lost its mid-stream fallback (H2), the `invoking tool` log line lost its args, a Stop was logged as an error, and text plus tool results could pass the 400 KB item limit. | Caught by Claude's inspection of the diff, before any commit claimed it. | Two failing tests (`b2b6954`) and one correction build (`64a72b3`). | **P104**. |
| M99 | The runner refused to resume the Codex session after the coordinator committed ("Checkout changed since the previous build"). | A second full build instead of a resume. | The correction ran as a fresh build from a self-contained order. | A commit between Codex runs means a fresh build: write every order self-contained. |
| M100 | Work order A prescribed `stream.onAbort` as the route's only abort source. @hono/node-server never cancels a body nobody started reading, so a reader who left before the response began was never noticed: the turn ran and billed to the end, `final` waited forever, the reply was never saved, the turn record stayed `streaming` and the heartbeat leaked for the life of the process. | Found by two review agents, each reproducing it on the repo's own hono and node-server; never shipped. | Work order C1: the route also honours `c.req.raw.signal`, and saves and ends the record before writing `final`. | **P106**. |
| M101 | The P2 tests mocked a mid-stream Stop as a thrown `AbortError`. Over HTTP/2 the real SDK ends an aborted event stream cleanly, so `converseStreamTurn` returned a normal result with no usage and the stopped turn settled $0. | Found by two review agents against the repo's SDK; never shipped. | Work order C2: an aborted stream throws; a call that returns after the Stop still counts as stopped. | **P106**: mock the dependency's real failure mode, and when unsure test both (throws, returns). |
| M102 | Work order A's stopped-turn estimate counted text only: not the attached files, the tool list, the re-sent tool arguments, nor a cut tool round's output. | A Stop could make the daily cost cap under-count by an amount the caller controls (security review S2); never shipped. | Work order C3: the estimate errs on the safe side, the ledger's own rule. | Under uncertainty, a spend estimate over-counts, and the order says so. |

| M104 | Work order B made the parser read `done: not_saved` and `errorCode`, and no order asked the page to use them. | An unsaved reply looked saved; typed errors were mangled into "Something went wrong: Something went wrong…"; never shipped. | Work order B2 (B2, B3) with browser tests. | **P107**. |
| M105 | `echo ===` in zsh: a word starting with `=` is `=`-expansion, so the command failed (`= not found`). | **None**. | Printed the files separately. | The M27/M28 family: quote or avoid a leading `=` in zsh. |
| M106 | The six-reviewer fan-out on Opus hit the account's session limit; five reviewers stopped mid-review (one had already written its report). | About an hour until the reset. | After the reset, the four without reports were resumed by id with their context intact, and all six reports landed. | **P108**. |
| M107 | Codex's workspace ran out of credits two minutes into work order C and left a partial, unverified edit of two files. | The Codex ladder ended for this step. | The partial diff was saved as a patch in the scratchpad and the clean commit restored; the build passed to Claude Fable at the same height (decision 49's rule, limit hit). | **P109**. |

| M109 | The redactor's `generic_api_key` pattern did not match a key written as a JSON property, so the new JSON egress (tool inputs and results) could pass the very secrets it names; the redaction test mocked the redactor, so nothing noticed. | Found by Codex's inspection; never shipped. | Work order D2, with a test on the REAL patterns. | Test a new egress control with the real patterns on the channel's real format (**P111**). |
| M110 | The stopped-turn estimate counted only the text a person saw for the streamed first call; a tool argument it was writing (a long document spec) was invisible and settled 0 output tokens. | Found by Codex's inspection; never shipped. | Work order D3. | Output a person never sees is still billed; count it. |
| M111 | The request-signal bridge (C1) aborted the turn but not the stream, so the non-model branches (a slash command, a media-only turn) still hung on a body nobody reads. | Found by Codex's inspection; never shipped. | Work order D4: `stream.abort()`. | Fix a hang at the transport, not branch by branch. |

#### Standing assumptions (continued)

| # | Assumption | Confidence and caveat |
|---|---|---|

|---|---|
| A Node ESM script outside the repository resolves packages next to ITSELF, not the working directory: `import '@aws-sdk/…'` fails with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. Use `createRequire(process.cwd() + '/')`. | This log, 6 |

| "codex exited 1" with no reply file: read `stdout.txt` — "Your workspace is out of credits" is the limit, not a build failure. | M107 |
